### plan-r5.md

# Plan: Sweep crash-orphaned engine-image leaves

## Goal

After a crash between installing an engine-image leaf and registering it, the next startup or shutdown cleanup removes that unreferenced regular file, and the same pass removes a crash-left `.atomic-<uuid>` temporary sibling. A leaf that is still referenced, still being issued, or living in a directory this process does not hold the application-data lock for stays on disk.

## MANDATE

### A crash between engine-image install and registration leaves an unreferenced image nothing ever removes

* **ID:** f-20261004-12 · **Status:** open · **Area:** native-fs · **Root:** - · **Entry:** build · **Blocked:** none
* **Filed from:** 4178af2d-b3e9-4852-871b-436172468f49
* **Where:** `src-tauri/src/main.rs:1768-1806` (`issue_engine_image_blocking`: `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` commits the UUID leaf, then `register_engine_image` persists it); `src-tauri/src/infra/fs.rs:1664-1724` (Unix `.atomic-<UUID>` temporary siblings, Windows equivalent near `:3787`).
* **Defect:** if the process dies after the image leaf is renamed into the EngineImages directory and before the registry save that registers it, the leaf has no registry entry and no `image_cleanup` intent. Startup and shutdown cleanup (`cleanup_engine_images`, `src-tauri/src/infra/path_authority/mod.rs:7235`) visit only recorded intents, and nothing enumerates that directory (the reason `d-20260905-09` gives). The same holds for an `.atomic-<UUID>` temporary sibling left by a crash during the copy. Each such crash leaves one file the user can neither see nor remove from the app.
* **Why it matters:** `.claude/rules/async-resource-invariants.md` (cleanup on every exit path, bounded retention). This finding is the crash half of `f-20260914-21`. The plan for that finding (`tasks/plans/2026-10-04-engine-image-cleanup-intent.md`, drain planner 2026-10-04) records an intent only when an in-process removal fails, and treats the crash window as out of scope.
* **Open question:** how is ownership of an unreferenced UUID leaf or `.atomic-*` sibling proven at startup, so that a sweep can delete it? A directory sweep is unsafe today: there is no single-instance guard (`f-20260922-07`), so a second instance's in-memory registry does not know the first instance's freshly registered or in-flight images, and its sweep would delete them. The candidates are (a) a sweep gated on the single-instance guard from `f-20260922-07` plus the issuance lease count, or (b) a write-ahead intent whose identity comes from the temporary inode before the rename (rename preserves the inode). Option (b) changes the atomic-replace protocol and still leaves temporary siblings from a crash before the intent is written.
* **Found by:** drain planner for `f-20260914-21` (Claude Code, 2026-10-04, session 4178af2d-b3e9-4852-871b-436172468f49), from the locate probe facts: no single-instance plugin (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs:2460`), no startup enumeration of the EngineImages directory. Not reproduced.
* **Prerequisite status (2026-10-08, drain session fa6786ed-3e23-4701-b1a3-05a243d9a80b):** the single-instance guard this entry names as missing now exists — `f-20260922-07`, commits `cd058c1a` and `870da73b`: `.setup` takes a non-blocking advisory lock (`instance.lock`) in every distinct per-user root the app writes (application data, configuration, local data) before credentials, the path authority or any window exist, and refuses a second process sharing any of them (`d-20261007-31`, `d-20261008-01`). The sweep may rely on one process per root except on a filesystem without advisory locks, where startup logs a warning and runs unguarded (`d-20261007-32`). The open question about proving ownership of an unreferenced leaf is otherwise unchanged.

The finding's `Where` line numbers are the filing-time citations. Live symbols are in Traced premises. This plan does not edit the finding.

Same-area consequence, not a publication id: `f-20261004-13` (registry growth during the unlocked copy can refuse both registration and the recovery intent, leaving an unrecorded UUID leaf). Its open question asks whether this sweep closes that orphan or a capacity reservation does. The sweep does. The adopting session closes `f-20261004-13` in the same ledger commit as this fix. Publication `ids` stay `["f-20261004-12"]`.

## Threat model and non-goals

Adversary and environment, frozen for round 1:

- One ChessFable process holds the advisory lock on the application-data directory that parents `engine-images`. A second process that shares that directory is refused at admission (`d-20261008-01`). The sweep may delete only while that lock is held by this process.
- `engine-images` contains only leaves this process writes: UUID-named image files and `.atomic-<uuid>` temps from `atomic_replace`. The only production writer is `issue_engine_image_blocking`.
- A crash, kill, or power loss can leave a renamed UUID leaf with no registry row and no `image_cleanup` intent, or an `.atomic-<uuid>` regular file whose rename never happened.
- Another root (configuration or local data) can be locked while the application-data filesystem cannot take an advisory lock. `InstanceAdmission::Owned` is then true and the application-data directory is not locked (`d-20261008-01`). The sweep must stay off in that case.
- An issuance lease can be in flight across the unlocked copy. The sweep must not delete that leaf.
- A name in the directory can be a directory, a symlink, or another non-regular node. Those are not deleted and not recursed into.
- Listing can exceed the sweep's cap. A partial list is not a safe deletion set.
- Registry paths can fail to parse. An unknown referenced name is not a license to delete.
- Logs must not emit `Error::diagnostic()` from this sweep (native path text).

Non-goals: changing `atomic_replace`, reserving registry capacity, merging with the macOS engine-launch sibling sweep, IPC or Specta changes, and deleting anything outside `engine-images`.

## Traced premises

Live at base `a2e8d4cd2999dc5f2b9eb87d1ff4fffc753d12de`. Line numbers are locate evidence, not obligations.

- `issue_engine_image_blocking` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs:1453`) takes `begin_engine_image_issuance` under `with_mut`, drops the authority lock, copies bytes, then `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` (`main.rs:1502`) and `register_engine_image` (`main.rs:1513`). The lease comment at `main.rs:1477` says the lease spans descriptor reads, the copy, registration, and error cleanup. `Drop` of `ActiveImageIssuanceLease` (`path_authority/mod.rs:4713`) decrements `active` without the authority mutex.
- `engine_image_error_after_cleanup` (`main.rs:1546`) unlinks first. It calls `record_engine_image_cleanup` only when removal fails and the error is neither a missing entry nor `CommittedDurabilityUncertain`. A crash before that call leaves no intent. `d-20261006-13` rejected a write-ahead intent because it still misses this crash window.
- Startup cleanup is `cleanup_engine_images(&image_dir, false)` inside `reconcile_engine_attachments` only when `startup` is true (`main.rs:700-706`), under `with_mut`, on `BLOCKING_GATEWAY` via `run_accepted_blocking`. Shutdown (`main.rs:1996-2010`) calls `seal_and_drain_engine_image_issuances` (`main.rs:2013-2026`, which seals then `wait_for_zero`) and then `cleanup_engine_images(&image_dir, false)` on the blocking gateway. Both call sites pass `seal: false` at the cleanup call. Shutdown is the pass that runs after leases have drained.
- `cleanup_engine_images` (`path_authority/mod.rs:8111`) returns immediately when `image_cleanup` is empty (`mod.rs:8119-8123`), after an optional durability retry. The non-empty walk (`mod.rs:8130-8178`) opens and unlinks only recorded intents whose parent equals `image_dir.path()` and whose opened identity agrees. Identity mismatch, missing, and `Conflict` drop the intent and do not unlink. Save and removal errors return `Err` (`mod.rs:8201-8217`). Nothing enumerates the directory.
- `ActiveImageIssuances.active` is a private `AtomicUsize` (`mod.rs:4681-4683`). `begin_engine_image_issuance` (`mod.rs:7899-7912`) increments it under `&mut self` and refuses when `attachments_sealed`. No public counter exists. A sweep that holds `&mut self` and observes zero cannot race a new `begin_engine_image_issuance`. A concurrent `Drop` only decrements.
- `InstanceLockGuard` (`mod.rs:2875`) stores only `_files`. `admit_instance_with_lock` (`mod.rs:2927`) tries application data first (`("application data", None)` at `mod.rs:2942`), then configuration and local data. An unsupported lock logs and continues (`mod.rs:2968-2974`). Empty `files` is `Unguarded`; otherwise `Owned` (`mod.rs:2978-2983`). A later duplicate identity is skipped, so application data is never skipped as a duplicate. `Owned` does not mean the application-data root itself locked.
- Setup (`main.rs:2285-2336`) matches admission, `app.manage`s an `Owned` guard, and continues on `Unguarded`. `HeldElsewhere` shows the dialog and `exit(0)`. `PathAuthority::open_for_app` runs after credentials. `SharedPathAuthority::install` (`shared.rs:45-51`) replaces the mutex contents. There is no `install` on `PathAuthority`. The setter has to run on the value before that move.
- `instance_admission_is_unconditional_and_precedes_shared_state` (`main.rs:4488-4541`) asserts the next statement starts with `match admission {`, exactly three `=>` arms, the `Unguarded` arm text `=> {}`, and the `Owned` body exactly `app.manage(guard);`. It also asserts the held-elsewhere arm still returns before `.credentials` and before `PathAuthority::open_for_app(`, and that `admit_instance(` occurs once. `body_at_indent` (`infra/blocking.rs:257`) stops at a `}` at the same indent, so the extracted arm excludes its closing brace.
- `AuthorizedDir::entries` (`mod.rs:1508-1517`) calls `read_directory_entries_at` with limit `None`. The sweep must not use it. `read_directory_entries_at` (`fs.rs:293` and the Windows entry at `fs.rs:409`) checks `check_directory_listing_bound` (`fs.rs:159`) before increment and before `keep` (`fs.rs:353-358`, Windows `fs.rs:3338-3341`). `count >= limit` returns `Err` and does not return a partial `Vec`. `MAX_DIRECTORY_LISTING_ENTRIES` is 4096 (`fs.rs:156`). `MAX_AUTHORITY_IDS` is 4096 (`mod.rs:126`). A full registry plus one unrecorded orphan (`f-20261004-13`) plus temps does not fit in 4096.
- `DirectoryEntryKind` is `Directory`, `RegularFile`, `Other` (`fs.rs:206-210`). Unix classification uses `SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` (`fs.rs:383-391`). Windows `enumerated_kind` (`fs.rs:3260-3267`) maps `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` to `Other` and a directory attribute to `Directory`. `open_leaf_identified` (`mod.rs:1305`) maps `ELOOP` to `Conflict`. `remove_leaf_identified` (`mod.rs:1525`) is identity-checked and not recursive.
- Unix and Windows `temp_name()` both use `format!(".atomic-{}", Uuid::new_v4())` (`fs.rs:2014`, `fs.rs:2896`).
- `provisional_attachments` is `BTreeSet<String>` of ids (`mod.rs:4484`), not paths. `retired_attachments` is `BTreeMap<String, Entry>` (`mod.rs:4747`). `Entry` wraps `StoredEntry` (`mod.rs:4523`). `StoredEntry.path` is `NativePath` (`mod.rs:4455-4460`). Retired rows are not authority after restart (`mod.rs:4485-4486`); their leaf names still name files that must not be swept.
- `EngineImageCleanupFixture::new` (`mod.rs:10241`) writes one unregistered UUID leaf. `reload_and_remove` (`mod.rs:10274`) removes via the intent. `fill_admission_bound` (`mod.rs:10292`) inserts 4096 in-memory cleanup rows that all point at that same leaf. Existing cleanup tests call `cleanup_engine_images` with no sweep flag. A default of false keeps them green.
- `instance_lock_classifies_per_root_outcomes` (`mod.rs:10027`) covers app-data locked with configuration unsupported (`[None, Some(unsupported), None]`, expects `Owned` and `_files.len() == 2`), all three unsupported (`Unguarded`), and a real error (`Err`). It does not cover application-data unsupported with another root locked. `unsupported` is `Errno::NOSYS` on unix and `ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` on Windows.
- `sweep_engine_launch_root` (`mod.rs:3503`) is macOS-only, lists another directory with limit `None`, and deletes unheld sibling lock directories. It returns `Err` on listing failure. It is a different operation.
- `scoped_test_removal_injector` (`fs.rs:1501`) and `RemovalFault` (`fs.rs:1455`) already fail `remove_entry_at`. `AppOwnedDefaultRoot::EngineImages` leaf is `"engine-images"` (`mod.rs:9847`). Production `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` into that root is only `issue_engine_image_blocking`. Other `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` callers use other roots (practice, credentials, tests).
- `PathAuthority` is constructed in one struct literal (`mod.rs:5556` region). A new field there defaults for every `open` / `open_for_app`. The field is on `PathAuthority`, not on `Registry`, so it is not serialized.

## Approach

### When the sweep runs

`cleanup_engine_images` gains one pass over `engine-images`, after the existing intent walk, on the same `&mut self` and the same `image_dir` the caller already passed. Call sites stay `cleanup_engine_images(&image_dir, false)`. No signature change, no new cancellation token, no IPC change.

The pass runs only when both gates hold:

1. This authority has been told the application-data lock is held. The flag lives on `PathAuthority`, default `false`, and is not serialized. `set_engine_image_sweep_permitted(bool)` is the only writer. Tests and every `open` / `open_for_app` stay false until setup sets it.
2. `self.active_image_issuances.active.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 0` while this method holds `&mut self`. Do not add a public counter. The field stays private. Same-module reads are enough.

If the flag is false, skip the pass with no new log. The unguarded-filesystem warning already exists. If the lease count is non-zero, skip the whole pass. `log::debug` is allowed; the production filter is Info, so users do not see it. Orphans wait for a later pass. Shutdown already seals, waits until the count is zero, then calls cleanup, and a sealed authority refuses new issuances, so shutdown is the pass that runs when startup skipped an in-flight issuance.

### What the sweep deletes

List with `read_directory_entries_at(image_dir.directory.as_file(), &CancellationToken::new(), Some(MAX_DIRECTORY_LISTING_ENTRIES.saturating_mul(2)), &mut |_| true)`. Do not call `AuthorizedDir::entries`. The limit is 8192. 8192 entries succeed. The 8193rd makes the call return `Err` and yields no partial list.

Build the protected-name set after the intent walk has updated `image_cleanup`. A name is protected when it is the normal leaf name of any `persistent`, `retired_attachments`, or `image_cleanup` entry. A normal leaf name is `file_name()` when that name is non-empty and not `.` or `..`. Match the leaf name, not the parent path. `provisional_attachments` are ids, not paths, and do not protect a file by themselves. If any of those three collections has an entry whose `to_path()` fails, or whose path has no normal leaf name, skip the whole pass, log the category, and delete nothing.

On a success path the existing intent walk leaves `image_cleanup` empty: an identity-matching intent is unlinked and dropped, and a parent or identity mismatch drops the row without unlinking. A successful `cleanup_engine_images` therefore does not keep an intent target on disk. Rows that remain are still part of the protected set. Today they remain only when the walk is about to return `Err`, and that path does not sweep.

On a listing `Err`, `log::warn` with `error.category()` only and delete nothing this pass. Do not fail `cleanup_engine_images` for a sweep listing failure.

For each listed entry, delete only when all of these hold:

- `kind` is `DirectoryEntryKind::RegularFile`.
- The name is either a UUID (`Uuid::parse_str` on the whole name) or a production temp: the name starts with `.atomic-` and the remainder parses as `Uuid`. No other prefix. This is the same `temp_name()` text on Unix and Windows.
- The name is not in the protected set.
- `open_leaf_identified` returns an identity equal to the listed `DirectoryEntry.identity`. Missing, `Conflict`, or a different identity skips that name and does not unlink. Any other open error logs the leaf name and `error.category()` and continues.
- `remove_leaf_identified` then unlinks that identity. One unlink error logs the leaf name and `error.category()` and does not abort the rest of the pass.

No recursive delete. No deletion of directories, symlinks, junctions, or `Other`. Do not log `diagnostic()`.

A dropped identity-mismatched intent no longer protects its leaf name. The file that remains is not the registered inode. If it is still an unreferenced UUID regular file, this pass may delete it. That is intentional.

### Failure and reconcile

Existing intent-walk errors still return `Err` and still fail the caller. The sweep does not run on that `Err` path. When `image_cleanup` is empty, today's early return must not skip the sweep: a pending durability retry still runs first; if that retry returns `Err`, return it and do not sweep; if it returns `Ok`, or there is nothing to retry, run the sweep and then return `Ok`. Sweep list and unlink failures are logged and left for the next startup or shutdown pass. They must not turn a successful reconcile into `Err` after attachments were already committed.

### Setup wiring and the source pin

`admit_instance_with_lock` records whether the application-data root's own lock returned `Ok`. Application data is the `None` root and is first, so a later duplicate cannot skip it. `InstanceLockGuard` exposes that bool through `app_data_locked()`. The field is false when that root's lock was unsupported, even if another root locked and the admission is `Owned`.

Setup becomes:

```rust
let app_data_locked = match admission {
    crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::Owned(guard) => {
        let app_data_locked = guard.app_data_locked();
        app.manage(guard);
        app_data_locked
    }
    crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::Unguarded => false,
    crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::HeldElsewhere => {
        // dialog, show, exit(0), return Ok(()) — unchanged
    }
};
```

After both `PathAuthority::open_for_app` bindings and before `pgn_path_authority.install(authority)`:

```rust
authority.set_engine_image_sweep_permitted(app_data_locked);
```

That call appears once in the source, outside the macOS `cfg` split, so the installed value carries the flag. `install` moves the value in. Setting the flag after `install` would not see it.

`instance_admission_is_unconditional_and_precedes_shared_state` is updated in the same change. Keep the admission-before-credentials and admission-before-`open_for_app` checks, the single `admit_instance(` count, the three arms, and the held-elsewhere dialog assertions. Change only what the new source requires:

- The next statement starts with `let app_data_locked = match admission {`.
- The search string passed to `body_at_indent` is `            let app_data_locked = match admission {`.
- The `Unguarded` variant line is `crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::Unguarded => false,`.
- The extracted `Owned` body, which still excludes the arm's closing brace, is the header plus `guard.app_data_locked()`, `app.manage(guard);`, and `app_data_locked`.
- The source contains `authority.set_engine_image_sweep_permitted(app_data_locked);` exactly once, after the last `PathAuthority::open_for_app(` and before `.install(authority)`.

Do not loosen the "admission precedes shared state" checks to make the new lines fit.

### Tests

Extend `instance_lock_classifies_per_root_outcomes`. Do not add a second classifier. The existing app-data-ok row asserts `app_data_locked() == true`. Add `[Some(unsupported), None, None]`: `Owned`, `app_data_locked() == false`, `_files.len() == 2`, and one warning that names the application-data root. `unsupported` stays `Errno::NOSYS` on unix and `ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` on Windows.

New cleanup tests use `EngineImageCleanupFixture` and the prefix `engine_image_cleanup_sweep`, so the filter `engine_image_cleanup` runs the old tests and the new ones. They must go red if the pass is removed or a gate is inverted:

- When the flag is set and the lease count is zero, an unreferenced UUID regular file and a `.atomic-<uuid>` regular file are removed. `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Ok`.
- A persistent registered engine-image leaf and a retired engine-image leaf are kept, including when the stored parent spelling differs from `image_dir.path()`. Returns `Ok`. The intent walk does not own those rows.
- An identity-matching `image_cleanup` intent is removed by the existing walk. The file is gone and cleanup returns `Ok`. Do not assert that the sweep keeps that file.
- A held issuance lease (`begin_engine_image_issuance`, count > 0) keeps the unreferenced UUID file and the temp. After the lease drops, a later cleanup removes them.
- With the flag left false, the unreferenced files stay. This is also why the existing fixture tests stay green.
- A non-regular `.atomic-*` or UUID name is left in place: a directory on every platform, and a symlink or junction using the existing platform fixture (`windows_test_junction` on Windows). An unrelated regular name is left in place.
- 4097 unreferenced UUID regular files are all removed. That is the case a 4096 cap would skip.
- 8193 directory entries: `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Ok` and deletes nothing, including an unreferenced UUID file that is one of those entries.
- Continuation of the sweep loop. Three unreferenced UUID regular files, no intents. A `RemovalInjector` fails the first two `BeforeTopOpen` calls and succeeds after that. `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Ok`, exactly one file is removed, and the other two remain. This is order-independent: stopping at the first error removes nothing, and stopping at the second error also removes nothing. `RemovalFault` fails every call, so it is not the fixture. The injector is `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` (`fs.rs` re-export around `4594`). The loop being proved is `cleanup_engine_images`, which is not `cfg`-split, so this test is the continuation proof for the Windows build of that function. Do not add an injector to `fs.rs`.
- Windows removal error, separate from the continuation proof. One orphan is opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, and another orphan is not. Cleanup returns `Ok` and the denied leaf remains. The free leaf is removed when the sweep reaches it. This shows a Windows sharing failure does not fail cleanup. It does not by itself prove that a later leaf is processed, because directory order is not a contract. The three-file injector test is that proof.
- Unreadable reference: one `persistent` entry whose `NativePath` is the other platform's variant, so `to_path()` returns `Err`, plus an unreferenced UUID regular file. Flag set, count zero. Returns `Ok`. The UUID file remains. The same assertion for a path `to_path()` accepts that has no normal leaf name (a root path).
- Intent-walk error skips the sweep. Flag set, one identity-matching `image_cleanup` intent, and a separate unreferenced UUID orphan. The intent's removal fails and a later removal of a different leaf would succeed. Unix: the one-shot injector, so the intent's `remove_entry_at` consumes the single `BeforeTopOpen` failure. Windows: set only the intent leaf's DACL to a protected allow ACE for the process user that grants `SYNCHRONIZE | GENERIC_READ | READ_CONTROL` and omits `DELETE`. Use the existing `set_test_target_security_descriptor` (`fs.rs:4376`). Re-export it with `pub(crate) use win::set_test_target_security_descriptor;` under `cfg(all(test, windows))`, next to the unix injector re-export. Do not change production removal and do not add an injector. The read open succeeds. The delete open inside `remove_leaf_identified` requests `TARGET_ACCESS`, which includes `DELETE`, and returns access denied. That is `Error::Io`: `windows_open_status_error` maps `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` through the default arm (`mod.rs:4075`), and that status is not a missing entry (`mod.rs:303`). A handle opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` is the wrong fixture. Sharing violation is `Error::Conflict` (`mod.rs:4074`), and the intent walk drops `Conflict` without recording a failure (`mod.rs:8157` and `mod.rs:8173`), then returns `Ok` (`mod.rs:8217`). The sweep would then run and could delete the orphan. `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Err`. The orphan remains. The intent leaf remains. A sweep that ran after the error would delete the orphan and fail this test.

The open-versus-listed identity mismatch is the same skip as the intent walk's disagreement branch. Do not add a listing hook to race it. Missing and `Conflict` from `open_leaf_identified` skip that name.

### Ledger bookkeeping for the adopter

Close `f-20261004-12` because the sweep removes the crash orphan. Close `f-20261004-13` in the same ledger commit because that orphan is an unreferenced UUID leaf and this pass removes it. Do not add a capacity reservation. Do not put `f-20261004-13` in publication ids.

Record the Decided autonomously entries below with `./scripts/findings.py record-decision` before implementation. The planner does not write `tasks/decisions.md`.

## Decisions and trade-offs

### Decided autonomously

Question: how is ownership of an unreferenced UUID leaf or `.atomic-<uuid>` sibling proved so a sweep can delete it?

Chosen: a directory sweep inside `cleanup_engine_images`, gated on the application-data advisory lock being held by this process and on the issuance count being zero.

Rejected: a write-ahead intent from the temporary inode before rename (`d-20261006-13` already rejected it for the in-process path; it also misses temps created before the intent write, and it changes atomic-replace). A capacity reservation for `f-20261004-13` (a second admission protocol; the sweep removes the same orphan on the existing startup and shutdown schedule). Sweeping whenever `InstanceAdmission::Owned` (configuration or local data can lock while application data did not). Sweeping while the issuance count is non-zero. Deleting by name without `open_leaf_identified` plus `remove_leaf_identified`. Recursive or non-regular deletion. Failing startup reconcile on a sweep error. Merging with `sweep_engine_launch_root`. A public issuance counter. Making `AuthorizedDir::entries` bounded for every caller. Logging `diagnostic()`.

Reason: the single-instance guard now exists, the lease already covers the unlocked copy, and the directory is app-owned. The sweep answers the finding's option (a) and the sibling finding's question without a new protocol.

Reversal path: remove the sweep pass and the setup setter, and restore the source pin's `match admission {` / `app.manage(guard);` / `Unguarded => {}` assertions. Intent cleanup stays.

Question: which lock bit enables the sweep?

Chosen: `app_data_locked` on the guard, true only when the application-data root's lock returned `Ok`, copied onto the authority with `set_engine_image_sweep_permitted` before `install`.

Rejected: treating `Owned` as sufficient.

Reason: `d-20261008-01` admits `Owned` when any root locked. Engine images live under application data.

Reversal path: stop recording the per-root bit and stop calling the setter.

Question: what listing limit does the sweep use?

Chosen: `MAX_DIRECTORY_LISTING_ENTRIES.saturating_mul(2)` (8192). On `Err`, delete nothing.

Rejected: 4096 (skips a full registry plus one orphan plus temps) and an unbounded list (a partial or huge listing is not a deletion set).

Reason: `check_directory_listing_bound` fails the whole call before it returns a `Vec`. 8192 covers 4096 registered ids plus 4096 orphans or temps.

Reversal path: change the limit expression and the 4097 / 8193 tests together.

## Risks / open questions

Known limits, accepted:

- If the application-data filesystem cannot take an advisory lock, the sweep never runs and crash orphans remain. `d-20261008-01` already starts that root unguarded and logs one warning. A second process could exist. Deleting would be unsafe.
- A directory with 8193 or more entries is skipped until a later pass sees fewer. 8192 covers a full 4096-id registry plus 4096 orphans or temps.
- An in-flight issuance defers the pass until the next cleanup after the count is zero. Shutdown drains the count first.
- The authority mutex is held for up to 8192 stat operations plus the unlinks. Intent cleanup already holds that mutex for its own unlinks. The work stays on the blocking gateway. No lock is held across `.await`.
- One unlink error leaves that leaf until the next pass. The rest of the pass still runs.
- An intent-walk `Err` skips the sweep until the next call. The existing error still reaches the caller.
- The open-versus-listed identity race is skipped per name and is not race-tested.

No product question remains. The finding's open question is answered by the decisions above.

## Not part of this task

- Co-located joiners, not planned: `f-20260915-02`, `f-20260920-12`, `f-20260920-17`, `f-20260922-06`. The adopting session confirms and works them only after this phase.
- Other open native-fs findings: `f-20260830-09`, `f-20261002-05`, `f-20261004-19`, `f-20261004-20`, `f-20261004-24`, `f-20261005-02`, `f-20261005-03`, `f-20261006-01`, `f-20261006-03`, `f-20261007-09`, `f-20261008-07`. `f-20261004-13` is the ledger close named above, not extra product behavior.
- Write-ahead intent, capacity reservation, atomic-replace changes, IPC, bindings, Specta, and the macOS engine-launch sweep. Rule 11 does not apply: `sweep_engine_launch_root` is a different directory, a different lock, and a different failure policy.
- UI. No verify-ui.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — Flag, sweep, setup pin, tests

Files: `src-tauri/src/infra/path_authority/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs`, and one test-only re-export in `src-tauri/src/infra/fs.rs`. Read `src-tauri/src/infra/path_authority/shared.rs` and `src-tauri/src/infra/blocking.rs`. Do not change those two. The `fs.rs` edit is only `pub(crate) use win::set_test_target_security_descriptor;` under `cfg(all(test, windows))`. Do not change production removal and do not add an injector.

Role: sensitive write leaf. One phase. The surface is the application-data lock, deletion, and the setup source pin.

Done when the obligations under Approach hold, the existing `engine_image_cleanup` tests still pass, and the proof below is green.

PROOF, three commands, from the repo root. `src-tauri` is the binary package `chessfable`. libtest takes one positional filter, so these stay separate. A filter that runs zero tests is a failed proof. The output must show a non-zero count and `ok` for every required test named in Approach. `generate_context!` (`main.rs:2245`) needs a local `dist/` directory; if cargo fails because it is missing, create an empty untracked `dist/` and rerun. Do not commit it. Do not treat `-- --list` as the proof. Final adopter gates are `pnpm gates:push -- --rust`. Windows proof for the adopter is `pnpm rust:windows:check` (GNU typecheck only). This phase does not change a Specta command, so it does not regenerate bindings.

```bash
cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --bin chessfable -- engine_image_cleanup
cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --bin chessfable -- instance_admission_is_unconditional_and_precedes_shared_state
cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --bin chessfable -- instance_lock_classifies_per_root_outcomes
```

## Reviews

Round 1 snapshot: `/tmp/drain-plan-drain-90a2aa88-slot1/build/plan-r1.md`. Lenses ran on that body. Raw reports stay in the run directory.

### R1-01 — unreadable reference has no deletion test

Claim: an implementation can ignore a `to_path()` failure and still pass every round-1 test (`review-plan` blocker, confidence 96). Live premise: `NativePath::to_path` returns `Err` for the other platform's variant (`path_authority/mod.rs:3787-3807`), and load already keeps undecodable persistent rows (`mod.rs:5577-5585`).

Witnesses: `review-plan` round 1, finding 1.

Disposition: Fix. Tests now require that an other-platform `NativePath` and a path with no normal leaf name each cause zero sweep deletions.

MANDATE obligation: the sweep must not delete a leaf it cannot prove is unreferenced.

Dependencies: none.

Closure: pending round 2. Closed in round 2 by `review-plan` and `review-platform-semantics` (both APPROVED). The unreadable-reference test is in the Tests list.

### R1-02 — round-1 packet omitted history fields

Claim: the round-1 prompt omitted PLAN-REVIEW HISTORY and CURRENT OPEN ISSUE IDS (`review-plan` blocker, confidence 100).

Witnesses: `review-plan` round 1, finding 2.

Disposition: Skip. Round 1 had no prior snapshot, no open issue, and no Reviews section. Those fields are prompt inputs. The closure prompt states history none and the round-1 ids. The plan's behavior does not change.

Reason: the contract's re-review block starts at round 2. Empty history was the round-1 state, not a missing obligation.

### R1-03 — one unlink failure must be one-shot and Windows-capable

Claim: `RemovalFault` fails every removal, and the injector is unix-only (`review-plan` should-fix, confidence 99; `review-platform-semantics` should-fix, confidence 98). `fs.rs` re-exports the injector under `cfg(all(test, unix))` at `fs.rs:4594`. Windows `remove_entry_at` (`fs.rs:5388`) has no injector.

Witnesses: `review-plan` round 1, finding 3; `review-platform-semantics` round 1, finding 1.

Disposition: Fix. Continuation stays required. Unix uses a one-shot `RemovalInjector`. Windows holds one file open without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`. `fs.rs` is not modified.

MANDATE obligation: one sweep unlink error does not abort the rest or fail cleanup.

Dependencies: none.

Closure: pending round 2. Closed in round 2 by `review-plan` and `review-platform-semantics`. Round 3 tightens the continuation fixture from one failure to two, because a single failure does not prove a later leaf was processed.

### R1-04 — an intent target is not kept by a successful cleanup

Claim: the existing walk unlinks a matching `image_cleanup` target before the sweep, and a removal failure returns `Err`, which skips the sweep (`review-plan` should-fix, confidence 96). `cleanup_engine_images` (`mod.rs:8147-8178` and `mod.rs:8217`).

Witnesses: `review-plan` round 1, finding 4.

Disposition: Fix. A successful cleanup still unlinks a matching intent. The sweep keeps persistent and retired leaf names, including a different parent spelling. Post-walk `image_cleanup` rows stay in the protected set, and on the success path that set is empty.

MANDATE obligation: referenced leaves stay; the existing intent walk stays.

Dependencies: none.

Closure: pending round 2. Closed in round 2 by `review-plan` and `review-platform-semantics`.

### R1-05 — intent-walk Err must be shown to skip the sweep

Claim: no test fails if the sweep runs before returning an intent-walk error (`review-tests` should-fix, confidence 96). Existing error tests leave the flag false.

Witnesses: `review-tests` round 1, finding 1.

Disposition: Fix. A permitted cleanup with one failing intent unlink and a separate orphan must return `Err` and leave the orphan. The intent consumes the single Unix failure, so a following sweep would delete the orphan.

MANDATE obligation: sweep errors do not fail reconcile; intent-walk errors still do, and they do not delete a different orphan on the way out.

Dependencies: R1-03 (the Unix fixture must fail once, or this test passes even if the sweep runs).

Closure: pending round 2. Closed in round 2 by `review-plan` and `review-tests`.

### R2-01 — one Windows failure does not prove a later leaf was processed

Claim: if the delete-denied leaf is enumerated last, a loop that returns after the first successful removal still passes the two-file Windows assertion (`review-tests` should-fix, round 2, confidence 96).

Witnesses: `review-tests` round 2, finding 1.

Disposition: Fix. The continuation proof is three UUID files and an injector that fails the first two `BeforeTopOpen` calls. Exactly one file is removed. That is order-independent and covers the shared `cleanup_engine_images` loop, which is not `cfg`-split. The Windows handle test only shows a sharing failure does not fail cleanup and does not delete the denied leaf. Directory order is not a contract, so that test is not the continuation proof.

MANDATE obligation: one sweep unlink error does not abort the rest.

Dependencies: R1-03.

Closure: pending round 3. Closed in round 3 by `review-plan`, `review-tests`, and `review-platform-semantics` (all APPROVED). The three-file injector is the continuation proof.

### R3-01 — a Windows sharing violation does not fail the intent walk

Claim: a handle opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` makes Windows removal return `Error::Conflict`, and the intent walk drops that without recording a failure, then returns `Ok`, so the sweep can delete the separate orphan (`review-plan` blocker, round 3, confidence 99). `windows_open_status_error` maps `STATUS_SHARING_VIOLATION` to `Conflict` (`mod.rs:4074`). `unlink_windows_child_at` opens with delete access (`fs.rs:3528`, called from `remove_entry_at` at `fs.rs:4151`). The walk drops `Conflict` at `mod.rs:8157` and `mod.rs:8173` and returns `Ok` at `mod.rs:8217` when no other failure was recorded.

Witnesses: `review-plan` round 3, finding 1. Round 4 restated this fix as not closed, and the other two lenses filed the same compile claim: `review-plan` round 4, finding 1; `review-tests` round 4, finding 1; `review-platform-semantics` round 4, finding 1.

Disposition: Fix. The Windows intent-skip fixture sets a protected allow ACE that grants read and omits `DELETE`, so the delete open returns access denied (`Error::Io`, not `Conflict`, not missing). The unix one-shot injector stays. The separate Windows sharing test stays the proof that a sharing failure does not fail the sweep. The only `fs.rs` change is a `cfg(all(test, windows))` re-export of the existing `set_test_target_security_descriptor`. This does not reopen R1-03: no injector is added and production removal is unchanged.

MANDATE obligation: an intent-walk error still fails cleanup and does not delete a different orphan.

Dependencies: R1-05. The unix half of that test stays fail-once. This replaces only its Windows fixture.

Closure: pending round 4. Not closed in round 4. All three lenses returned REVISE and said `pub(crate) use` of the `pub(super)` helper is E0364. Measured on rustc 1.98.0, the channel in `rust-toolchain.toml`: `pub(crate) use` of a `pub(super)` item from the parent module compiles (`/tmp/drain-plan-drain-90a2aa88-slot1/build/e0364.rs`, exit 0). E0364 is `pub use` of that item (`e0364-pub.rs`). A private re-export is E0603 (`e0364-private.rs`). The plan text is unchanged. Closure pending the evidence check.

### Round 1 lenses with no findings

`review-correctness`, `review-error-handling`, `review-minimalism`, and `review-tauri-security` approved. `review-chess-semantics`, `review-engine-protocol`, `review-ipc-contract`, `review-persisted-state`, and `review-pgn-index` reported NOT APPLICABLE and approved. `review-code-quality` and `review-root-cause` are `plan-review: false` and were not launched.

### plan-r6.md

# Plan: Sweep crash-orphaned engine-image leaves

## Goal

After a crash between installing an engine-image leaf and registering it, the next startup or shutdown cleanup removes that unreferenced regular file, and the same pass removes a crash-left `.atomic-<uuid>` temporary sibling. A leaf that is still referenced, still being issued, or living in a directory this process does not hold the application-data lock for stays on disk.

## MANDATE

### A crash between engine-image install and registration leaves an unreferenced image nothing ever removes

* **ID:** f-20261004-12 · **Status:** open · **Area:** native-fs · **Root:** - · **Entry:** build · **Blocked:** none
* **Filed from:** 4178af2d-b3e9-4852-871b-436172468f49
* **Where:** `src-tauri/src/main.rs:1768-1806` (`issue_engine_image_blocking`: `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` commits the UUID leaf, then `register_engine_image` persists it); `src-tauri/src/infra/fs.rs:1664-1724` (Unix `.atomic-<UUID>` temporary siblings, Windows equivalent near `:3787`).
* **Defect:** if the process dies after the image leaf is renamed into the EngineImages directory and before the registry save that registers it, the leaf has no registry entry and no `image_cleanup` intent. Startup and shutdown cleanup (`cleanup_engine_images`, `src-tauri/src/infra/path_authority/mod.rs:7235`) visit only recorded intents, and nothing enumerates that directory (the reason `d-20260905-09` gives). The same holds for an `.atomic-<UUID>` temporary sibling left by a crash during the copy. Each such crash leaves one file the user can neither see nor remove from the app.
* **Why it matters:** `.claude/rules/async-resource-invariants.md` (cleanup on every exit path, bounded retention). This finding is the crash half of `f-20260914-21`. The plan for that finding (`tasks/plans/2026-10-04-engine-image-cleanup-intent.md`, drain planner 2026-10-04) records an intent only when an in-process removal fails, and treats the crash window as out of scope.
* **Open question:** how is ownership of an unreferenced UUID leaf or `.atomic-*` sibling proven at startup, so that a sweep can delete it? A directory sweep is unsafe today: there is no single-instance guard (`f-20260922-07`), so a second instance's in-memory registry does not know the first instance's freshly registered or in-flight images, and its sweep would delete them. The candidates are (a) a sweep gated on the single-instance guard from `f-20260922-07` plus the issuance lease count, or (b) a write-ahead intent whose identity comes from the temporary inode before the rename (rename preserves the inode). Option (b) changes the atomic-replace protocol and still leaves temporary siblings from a crash before the intent is written.
* **Found by:** drain planner for `f-20260914-21` (Claude Code, 2026-10-04, session 4178af2d-b3e9-4852-871b-436172468f49), from the locate probe facts: no single-instance plugin (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs:2460`), no startup enumeration of the EngineImages directory. Not reproduced.
* **Prerequisite status (2026-10-08, drain session fa6786ed-3e23-4701-b1a3-05a243d9a80b):** the single-instance guard this entry names as missing now exists — `f-20260922-07`, commits `cd058c1a` and `870da73b`: `.setup` takes a non-blocking advisory lock (`instance.lock`) in every distinct per-user root the app writes (application data, configuration, local data) before credentials, the path authority or any window exist, and refuses a second process sharing any of them (`d-20261007-31`, `d-20261008-01`). The sweep may rely on one process per root except on a filesystem without advisory locks, where startup logs a warning and runs unguarded (`d-20261007-32`). The open question about proving ownership of an unreferenced leaf is otherwise unchanged.

The finding's `Where` line numbers are the filing-time citations. Live symbols are in Traced premises. This plan does not edit the finding.

Same-area consequence, not a publication id: `f-20261004-13` (registry growth during the unlocked copy can refuse both registration and the recovery intent, leaving an unrecorded UUID leaf). Its open question asks whether this sweep closes that orphan or a capacity reservation does. The sweep does. The adopting session closes `f-20261004-13` in the same ledger commit as this fix. Publication `ids` stay `["f-20261004-12"]`.

## Threat model and non-goals

Adversary and environment, frozen for round 1:

- One ChessFable process holds the advisory lock on the application-data directory that parents `engine-images`. A second process that shares that directory is refused at admission (`d-20261008-01`). The sweep may delete only while that lock is held by this process.
- `engine-images` contains only leaves this process writes: UUID-named image files and `.atomic-<uuid>` temps from `atomic_replace`. The only production writer is `issue_engine_image_blocking`.
- A crash, kill, or power loss can leave a renamed UUID leaf with no registry row and no `image_cleanup` intent, or an `.atomic-<uuid>` regular file whose rename never happened.
- Another root (configuration or local data) can be locked while the application-data filesystem cannot take an advisory lock. `InstanceAdmission::Owned` is then true and the application-data directory is not locked (`d-20261008-01`). The sweep must stay off in that case.
- An issuance lease can be in flight across the unlocked copy. The sweep must not delete that leaf.
- A name in the directory can be a directory, a symlink, or another non-regular node. Those are not deleted and not recursed into.
- Listing can exceed the sweep's cap. A partial list is not a safe deletion set.
- Registry paths can fail to parse. An unknown referenced name is not a license to delete.
- Logs must not emit `Error::diagnostic()` from this sweep (native path text).

Non-goals: changing `atomic_replace`, reserving registry capacity, merging with the macOS engine-launch sibling sweep, IPC or Specta changes, and deleting anything outside `engine-images`.

## Traced premises

Live at base `a2e8d4cd2999dc5f2b9eb87d1ff4fffc753d12de`. Line numbers are locate evidence, not obligations.

- `issue_engine_image_blocking` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs:1453`) takes `begin_engine_image_issuance` under `with_mut`, drops the authority lock, copies bytes, then `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` (`main.rs:1502`) and `register_engine_image` (`main.rs:1513`). The lease comment at `main.rs:1477` says the lease spans descriptor reads, the copy, registration, and error cleanup. `Drop` of `ActiveImageIssuanceLease` (`path_authority/mod.rs:4713`) decrements `active` without the authority mutex.
- `engine_image_error_after_cleanup` (`main.rs:1546`) unlinks first. It calls `record_engine_image_cleanup` only when removal fails and the error is neither a missing entry nor `CommittedDurabilityUncertain`. A crash before that call leaves no intent. `d-20261006-13` rejected a write-ahead intent because it still misses this crash window.
- Startup cleanup is `cleanup_engine_images(&image_dir, false)` inside `reconcile_engine_attachments` only when `startup` is true (`main.rs:700-706`), under `with_mut`, on `BLOCKING_GATEWAY` via `run_accepted_blocking`. Shutdown (`main.rs:1996-2010`) calls `seal_and_drain_engine_image_issuances` (`main.rs:2013-2026`, which seals then `wait_for_zero`) and then `cleanup_engine_images(&image_dir, false)` on the blocking gateway. Both call sites pass `seal: false` at the cleanup call. Shutdown is the pass that runs after leases have drained.
- `cleanup_engine_images` (`path_authority/mod.rs:8111`) returns immediately when `image_cleanup` is empty (`mod.rs:8119-8123`), after an optional durability retry. The non-empty walk (`mod.rs:8130-8178`) opens and unlinks only recorded intents whose parent equals `image_dir.path()` and whose opened identity agrees. Identity mismatch, missing, and `Conflict` drop the intent and do not unlink. Save and removal errors return `Err` (`mod.rs:8201-8217`). Nothing enumerates the directory.
- `ActiveImageIssuances.active` is a private `AtomicUsize` (`mod.rs:4681-4683`). `begin_engine_image_issuance` (`mod.rs:7899-7912`) increments it under `&mut self` and refuses when `attachments_sealed`. No public counter exists. A sweep that holds `&mut self` and observes zero cannot race a new `begin_engine_image_issuance`. A concurrent `Drop` only decrements.
- `InstanceLockGuard` (`mod.rs:2875`) stores only `_files`. `admit_instance_with_lock` (`mod.rs:2927`) tries application data first (`("application data", None)` at `mod.rs:2942`), then configuration and local data. An unsupported lock logs and continues (`mod.rs:2968-2974`). Empty `files` is `Unguarded`; otherwise `Owned` (`mod.rs:2978-2983`). A later duplicate identity is skipped, so application data is never skipped as a duplicate. `Owned` does not mean the application-data root itself locked.
- Setup (`main.rs:2285-2336`) matches admission, `app.manage`s an `Owned` guard, and continues on `Unguarded`. `HeldElsewhere` shows the dialog and `exit(0)`. `PathAuthority::open_for_app` runs after credentials. `SharedPathAuthority::install` (`shared.rs:45-51`) replaces the mutex contents. There is no `install` on `PathAuthority`. The setter has to run on the value before that move.
- `instance_admission_is_unconditional_and_precedes_shared_state` (`main.rs:4488-4541`) asserts the next statement starts with `match admission {`, exactly three `=>` arms, the `Unguarded` arm text `=> {}`, and the `Owned` body exactly `app.manage(guard);`. It also asserts the held-elsewhere arm still returns before `.credentials` and before `PathAuthority::open_for_app(`, and that `admit_instance(` occurs once. `body_at_indent` (`infra/blocking.rs:257`) stops at a `}` at the same indent, so the extracted arm excludes its closing brace.
- `AuthorizedDir::entries` (`mod.rs:1508-1517`) calls `read_directory_entries_at` with limit `None`. The sweep must not use it. `read_directory_entries_at` (`fs.rs:293` and the Windows entry at `fs.rs:409`) checks `check_directory_listing_bound` (`fs.rs:159`) before increment and before `keep` (`fs.rs:353-358`, Windows `fs.rs:3338-3341`). `count >= limit` returns `Err` and does not return a partial `Vec`. `MAX_DIRECTORY_LISTING_ENTRIES` is 4096 (`fs.rs:156`). `MAX_AUTHORITY_IDS` is 4096 (`mod.rs:126`). A full registry plus one unrecorded orphan (`f-20261004-13`) plus temps does not fit in 4096.
- `DirectoryEntryKind` is `Directory`, `RegularFile`, `Other` (`fs.rs:206-210`). Unix classification uses `SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` (`fs.rs:383-391`). Windows `enumerated_kind` (`fs.rs:3260-3267`) maps `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` to `Other` and a directory attribute to `Directory`. `open_leaf_identified` (`mod.rs:1305`) maps `ELOOP` to `Conflict`. `remove_leaf_identified` (`mod.rs:1525`) is identity-checked and not recursive.
- Unix and Windows `temp_name()` both use `format!(".atomic-{}", Uuid::new_v4())` (`fs.rs:2014`, `fs.rs:2896`).
- `provisional_attachments` is `BTreeSet<String>` of ids (`mod.rs:4484`), not paths. `retired_attachments` is `BTreeMap<String, Entry>` (`mod.rs:4747`). `Entry` wraps `StoredEntry` (`mod.rs:4523`). `StoredEntry.path` is `NativePath` (`mod.rs:4455-4460`). Retired rows are not authority after restart (`mod.rs:4485-4486`); their leaf names still name files that must not be swept.
- `EngineImageCleanupFixture::new` (`mod.rs:10241`) writes one unregistered UUID leaf. `reload_and_remove` (`mod.rs:10274`) removes via the intent. `fill_admission_bound` (`mod.rs:10292`) inserts 4096 in-memory cleanup rows that all point at that same leaf. Existing cleanup tests call `cleanup_engine_images` with no sweep flag. A default of false keeps them green.
- `instance_lock_classifies_per_root_outcomes` (`mod.rs:10027`) covers app-data locked with configuration unsupported (`[None, Some(unsupported), None]`, expects `Owned` and `_files.len() == 2`), all three unsupported (`Unguarded`), and a real error (`Err`). It does not cover application-data unsupported with another root locked. `unsupported` is `Errno::NOSYS` on unix and `ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` on Windows.
- `sweep_engine_launch_root` (`mod.rs:3503`) is macOS-only, lists another directory with limit `None`, and deletes unheld sibling lock directories. It returns `Err` on listing failure. It is a different operation.
- `scoped_test_removal_injector` (`fs.rs:1501`) and `RemovalFault` (`fs.rs:1455`) already fail `remove_entry_at`. `AppOwnedDefaultRoot::EngineImages` leaf is `"engine-images"` (`mod.rs:9847`). Production `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` into that root is only `issue_engine_image_blocking`. Other `atomic_replace_leaf_identified` callers use other roots (practice, credentials, tests).
- `PathAuthority` is constructed in one struct literal (`mod.rs:5556` region). A new field there defaults for every `open` / `open_for_app`. The field is on `PathAuthority`, not on `Registry`, so it is not serialized.

## Approach

### When the sweep runs

`cleanup_engine_images` gains one pass over `engine-images`, after the existing intent walk, on the same `&mut self` and the same `image_dir` the caller already passed. Call sites stay `cleanup_engine_images(&image_dir, false)`. No signature change, no new cancellation token, no IPC change.

The pass runs only when both gates hold:

1. This authority has been told the application-data lock is held. The flag lives on `PathAuthority`, default `false`, and is not serialized. `set_engine_image_sweep_permitted(bool)` is the only writer. Tests and every `open` / `open_for_app` stay false until setup sets it.
2. `self.active_image_issuances.active.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 0` while this method holds `&mut self`. Do not add a public counter. The field stays private. Same-module reads are enough.

If the flag is false, skip the pass with no new log. The unguarded-filesystem warning already exists. If the lease count is non-zero, skip the whole pass. `log::debug` is allowed; the production filter is Info, so users do not see it. Orphans wait for a later pass. Shutdown already seals, waits until the count is zero, then calls cleanup, and a sealed authority refuses new issuances, so shutdown is the pass that runs when startup skipped an in-flight issuance.

### What the sweep deletes

List with `read_directory_entries_at(image_dir.directory.as_file(), &CancellationToken::new(), Some(MAX_DIRECTORY_LISTING_ENTRIES.saturating_mul(2)), &mut |_| true)`. Do not call `AuthorizedDir::entries`. The limit is 8192. 8192 entries succeed. The 8193rd makes the call return `Err` and yields no partial list.

Build the protected-name set after the intent walk has updated `image_cleanup`. A name is protected when it is the normal leaf name of any `persistent`, `retired_attachments`, or `image_cleanup` entry. A normal leaf name is `file_name()` when that name is non-empty and not `.` or `..`. Match the leaf name, not the parent path. `provisional_attachments` are ids, not paths, and do not protect a file by themselves. If any of those three collections has an entry whose `to_path()` fails, or whose path has no normal leaf name, skip the whole pass, log the category, and delete nothing.

On a success path the existing intent walk leaves `image_cleanup` empty: an identity-matching intent is unlinked and dropped, and a parent or identity mismatch drops the row without unlinking. A successful `cleanup_engine_images` therefore does not keep an intent target on disk. Rows that remain are still part of the protected set. Today they remain only when the walk is about to return `Err`, and that path does not sweep.

On a listing `Err`, `log::warn` with `error.category()` only and delete nothing this pass. Do not fail `cleanup_engine_images` for a sweep listing failure.

For each listed entry, delete only when all of these hold:

- `kind` is `DirectoryEntryKind::RegularFile`.
- The name is either a UUID (`Uuid::parse_str` on the whole name) or a production temp: the name starts with `.atomic-` and the remainder parses as `Uuid`. No other prefix. This is the same `temp_name()` text on Unix and Windows.
- The name is not in the protected set.
- `open_leaf_identified` returns an identity equal to the listed `DirectoryEntry.identity`. Missing, `Conflict`, or a different identity skips that name and does not unlink. Any other open error logs the leaf name and `error.category()` and continues.
- `remove_leaf_identified` then unlinks that identity. One unlink error logs the leaf name and `error.category()` and does not abort the rest of the pass.

No recursive delete. No deletion of directories, symlinks, junctions, or `Other`. Do not log `diagnostic()`.

A dropped identity-mismatched intent no longer protects its leaf name. The file that remains is not the registered inode. If it is still an unreferenced UUID regular file, this pass may delete it. That is intentional.

### Failure and reconcile

Existing intent-walk errors still return `Err` and still fail the caller. The sweep does not run on that `Err` path. When `image_cleanup` is empty, today's early return must not skip the sweep: a pending durability retry still runs first; if that retry returns `Err`, return it and do not sweep; if it returns `Ok`, or there is nothing to retry, run the sweep and then return `Ok`. Sweep list and unlink failures are logged and left for the next startup or shutdown pass. They must not turn a successful reconcile into `Err` after attachments were already committed.

### Setup wiring and the source pin

`admit_instance_with_lock` records whether the application-data root's own lock returned `Ok`. Application data is the `None` root and is first, so a later duplicate cannot skip it. `InstanceLockGuard` exposes that bool through `app_data_locked()`. The field is false when that root's lock was unsupported, even if another root locked and the admission is `Owned`.

Setup becomes:

```rust
let app_data_locked = match admission {
    crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::Owned(guard) => {
        let app_data_locked = guard.app_data_locked();
        app.manage(guard);
        app_data_locked
    }
    crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::Unguarded => false,
    crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::HeldElsewhere => {
        // dialog, show, exit(0), return Ok(()) — unchanged
    }
};
```

After both `PathAuthority::open_for_app` bindings and before `pgn_path_authority.install(authority)`:

```rust
authority.set_engine_image_sweep_permitted(app_data_locked);
```

That call appears once in the source, outside the macOS `cfg` split, so the installed value carries the flag. `install` moves the value in. Setting the flag after `install` would not see it.

`instance_admission_is_unconditional_and_precedes_shared_state` is updated in the same change. Keep the admission-before-credentials and admission-before-`open_for_app` checks, the single `admit_instance(` count, the three arms, and the held-elsewhere dialog assertions. Change only what the new source requires:

- The next statement starts with `let app_data_locked = match admission {`.
- The search string passed to `body_at_indent` is `            let app_data_locked = match admission {`.
- The `Unguarded` variant line is `crate::infra::path_authority::InstanceAdmission::Unguarded => false,`.
- The extracted `Owned` body, which still excludes the arm's closing brace, is the header plus `guard.app_data_locked()`, `app.manage(guard);`, and `app_data_locked`.
- The source contains `authority.set_engine_image_sweep_permitted(app_data_locked);` exactly once, after the last `PathAuthority::open_for_app(` and before `.install(authority)`.

Do not loosen the "admission precedes shared state" checks to make the new lines fit.

### Tests

Extend `instance_lock_classifies_per_root_outcomes`. Do not add a second classifier. The existing app-data-ok row asserts `app_data_locked() == true`. Add `[Some(unsupported), None, None]`: `Owned`, `app_data_locked() == false`, `_files.len() == 2`, and one warning that names the application-data root. `unsupported` stays `Errno::NOSYS` on unix and `ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` on Windows.

New cleanup tests use `EngineImageCleanupFixture` and the prefix `engine_image_cleanup_sweep`, so the filter `engine_image_cleanup` runs the old tests and the new ones. They must go red if the pass is removed or a gate is inverted:

- When the flag is set and the lease count is zero, an unreferenced UUID regular file and a `.atomic-<uuid>` regular file are removed. `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Ok`.
- A persistent registered engine-image leaf and a retired engine-image leaf are kept, including when the stored parent spelling differs from `image_dir.path()`. Returns `Ok`. The intent walk does not own those rows.
- An identity-matching `image_cleanup` intent is removed by the existing walk. The file is gone and cleanup returns `Ok`. Do not assert that the sweep keeps that file.
- A held issuance lease (`begin_engine_image_issuance`, count > 0) keeps the unreferenced UUID file and the temp. After the lease drops, a later cleanup removes them.
- With the flag left false, the unreferenced files stay. This is also why the existing fixture tests stay green.
- A non-regular `.atomic-*` or UUID name is left in place: a directory on every platform, and a symlink or junction using the existing platform fixture (`windows_test_junction` on Windows). An unrelated regular name is left in place.
- 4097 unreferenced UUID regular files are all removed. That is the case a 4096 cap would skip.
- 8193 directory entries: `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Ok` and deletes nothing, including an unreferenced UUID file that is one of those entries.
- Continuation of the sweep loop. Three unreferenced UUID regular files, no intents. A `RemovalInjector` fails the first two `BeforeTopOpen` calls and succeeds after that. `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Ok`, exactly one file is removed, and the other two remain. This is order-independent: stopping at the first error removes nothing, and stopping at the second error also removes nothing. `RemovalFault` fails every call, so it is not the fixture. The injector is `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` (`fs.rs` re-export around `4594`). The loop being proved is `cleanup_engine_images`, which is not `cfg`-split, so this test is the continuation proof for the Windows build of that function. Do not add an injector to `fs.rs`.
- Windows removal error, separate from the continuation proof. One orphan is opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, and another orphan is not. Cleanup returns `Ok` and the denied leaf remains. The free leaf is removed when the sweep reaches it. This shows a Windows sharing failure does not fail cleanup. It does not by itself prove that a later leaf is processed, because directory order is not a contract. The three-file injector test is that proof.
- Unreadable reference: one `persistent` entry whose `NativePath` is the other platform's variant, so `to_path()` returns `Err`, plus an unreferenced UUID regular file. Flag set, count zero. Returns `Ok`. The UUID file remains. The same assertion for a path `to_path()` accepts that has no normal leaf name (a root path).
- Intent-walk error skips the sweep. Flag set, one identity-matching `image_cleanup` intent, and a separate unreferenced UUID orphan. The intent's removal fails and a later removal of a different leaf would succeed. Unix: the one-shot injector, so the intent's `remove_entry_at` consumes the single `BeforeTopOpen` failure. Windows: set only the intent leaf's DACL to a protected allow ACE for the process user that grants `SYNCHRONIZE | GENERIC_READ | READ_CONTROL` and omits `DELETE`. Use the existing `set_test_target_security_descriptor` (`fs.rs:4376`). Change that helper from `pub(super)` to `pub(crate)`. It stays `#[cfg(test)]`. Then re-export it with `pub(crate) use win::set_test_target_security_descriptor;` under `cfg(all(test, windows))`, next to the unix injector re-export. A `pub(crate) use` of the unchanged `pub(super)` item is E0364, because `win` is nested under `infra::fs` (rustc 1.98.0). Do not change the helper's body, do not change production removal, and do not add an injector. The read open succeeds. The delete open inside `remove_leaf_identified` requests `TARGET_ACCESS`, which includes `DELETE`, and returns access denied. That is `Error::Io`: `windows_open_status_error` maps `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` through the default arm (`mod.rs:4075`), and that status is not a missing entry (`mod.rs:303`). A handle opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` is the wrong fixture. Sharing violation is `Error::Conflict` (`mod.rs:4074`), and the intent walk drops `Conflict` without recording a failure (`mod.rs:8157` and `mod.rs:8173`), then returns `Ok` (`mod.rs:8217`). The sweep would then run and could delete the orphan. `cleanup_engine_images` returns `Err`. The orphan remains. The intent leaf remains. A sweep that ran after the error would delete the orphan and fail this test.

The open-versus-listed identity mismatch is the same skip as the intent walk's disagreement branch. Do not add a listing hook to race it. Missing and `Conflict` from `open_leaf_identified` skip that name.

### Ledger bookkeeping for the adopter

Close `f-20261004-12` because the sweep removes the crash orphan. Close `f-20261004-13` in the same ledger commit because that orphan is an unreferenced UUID leaf and this pass removes it. Do not add a capacity reservation. Do not put `f-20261004-13` in publication ids.

Record the Decided autonomously entries below with `./scripts/findings.py record-decision` before implementation. The planner does not write `tasks/decisions.md`.

## Decisions and trade-offs

### Decided autonomously

Question: how is ownership of an unreferenced UUID leaf or `.atomic-<uuid>` sibling proved so a sweep can delete it?

Chosen: a directory sweep inside `cleanup_engine_images`, gated on the application-data advisory lock being held by this process and on the issuance count being zero.

Rejected: a write-ahead intent from the temporary inode before rename (`d-20261006-13` already rejected it for the in-process path; it also misses temps created before the intent write, and it changes atomic-replace). A capacity reservation for `f-20261004-13` (a second admission protocol; the sweep removes the same orphan on the existing startup and shutdown schedule). Sweeping whenever `InstanceAdmission::Owned` (configuration or local data can lock while application data did not). Sweeping while the issuance count is non-zero. Deleting by name without `open_leaf_identified` plus `remove_leaf_identified`. Recursive or non-regular deletion. Failing startup reconcile on a sweep error. Merging with `sweep_engine_launch_root`. A public issuance counter. Making `AuthorizedDir::entries` bounded for every caller. Logging `diagnostic()`.

Reason: the single-instance guard now exists, the lease already covers the unlocked copy, and the directory is app-owned. The sweep answers the finding's option (a) and the sibling finding's question without a new protocol.

Reversal path: remove the sweep pass and the setup setter, and restore the source pin's `match admission {` / `app.manage(guard);` / `Unguarded => {}` assertions. Intent cleanup stays.

Question: which lock bit enables the sweep?

Chosen: `app_data_locked` on the guard, true only when the application-data root's lock returned `Ok`, copied onto the authority with `set_engine_image_sweep_permitted` before `install`.

Rejected: treating `Owned` as sufficient.

Reason: `d-20261008-01` admits `Owned` when any root locked. Engine images live under application data.

Reversal path: stop recording the per-root bit and stop calling the setter.

Question: what listing limit does the sweep use?

Chosen: `MAX_DIRECTORY_LISTING_ENTRIES.saturating_mul(2)` (8192). On `Err`, delete nothing.

Rejected: 4096 (skips a full registry plus one orphan plus temps) and an unbounded list (a partial or huge listing is not a deletion set).

Reason: `check_directory_listing_bound` fails the whole call before it returns a `Vec`. 8192 covers 4096 registered ids plus 4096 orphans or temps.

Reversal path: change the limit expression and the 4097 / 8193 tests together.

## Risks / open questions

Known limits, accepted:

- If the application-data filesystem cannot take an advisory lock, the sweep never runs and crash orphans remain. `d-20261008-01` already starts that root unguarded and logs one warning. A second process could exist. Deleting would be unsafe.
- A directory with 8193 or more entries is skipped until a later pass sees fewer. 8192 covers a full 4096-id registry plus 4096 orphans or temps.
- An in-flight issuance defers the pass until the next cleanup after the count is zero. Shutdown drains the count first.
- The authority mutex is held for up to 8192 stat operations plus the unlinks. Intent cleanup already holds that mutex for its own unlinks. The work stays on the blocking gateway. No lock is held across `.await`.
- One unlink error leaves that leaf until the next pass. The rest of the pass still runs.
- An intent-walk `Err` skips the sweep until the next call. The existing error still reaches the caller.
- The open-versus-listed identity race is skipped per name and is not race-tested.

No product question remains. The finding's open question is answered by the decisions above.

## Not part of this task

- Co-located joiners, not planned: `f-20260915-02`, `f-20260920-12`, `f-20260920-17`, `f-20260922-06`. The adopting session confirms and works them only after this phase.
- Other open native-fs findings: `f-20260830-09`, `f-20261002-05`, `f-20261004-19`, `f-20261004-20`, `f-20261004-24`, `f-20261005-02`, `f-20261005-03`, `f-20261006-01`, `f-20261006-03`, `f-20261007-09`, `f-20261008-07`. `f-20261004-13` is the ledger close named above, not extra product behavior.
- Write-ahead intent, capacity reservation, atomic-replace changes, IPC, bindings, Specta, and the macOS engine-launch sweep. Rule 11 does not apply: `sweep_engine_launch_root` is a different directory, a different lock, and a different failure policy.
- UI. No verify-ui.

Files: `src-tauri/src/infra/path_authority/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs`, and the test-helper visibility plus its re-export in `src-tauri/src/infra/fs.rs`. Read `src-tauri/src/infra/path_authority/shared.rs` and `src-tauri/src/infra/blocking.rs`. Do not change those two. The `fs.rs` edits are `pub(super)` to `pub(crate)` on the existing `#[cfg(test)]` `set_test_target_security_descriptor`, and `pub(crate) use win::set_test_target_security_descriptor;` under `cfg(all(test, windows))`. Do not change the helper's body, do not change production removal, and do not add an injector.

Role: sensitive write leaf. One phase. The surface is the application-data lock, deletion, and the setup source pin.

Done when the obligations under Approach hold, the existing `engine_image_cleanup` tests still pass, and the proof below is green.

PROOF, three commands, from the repo root. `src-tauri` is the binary package `chessfable`. libtest takes one positional filter, so these stay separate. A filter that runs zero tests is a failed proof. The output must show a non-zero count and `ok` for every required test named in Approach. `generate_context!` (`main.rs:2245`) needs a local `dist/` directory; if cargo fails because it is missing, create an empty untracked `dist/` and rerun. The Windows handle test only shows a sharing failure does not fail cleanup and does not delete the denied leaf. Directory order is not a contract, so that test is not the continuation proof.

### R3-01 — a Windows sharing violation does not fail the intent walk

Claim: a handle opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` makes Windows removal return `Error::Conflict`, and the intent walk drops that without recording a failure, then returns `Ok`, so the sweep can delete the separate orphan (`review-plan` blocker, round 3, confidence 99). `windows_open_status_error` maps `STATUS_SHARING_VIOLATION` to `Conflict` (`mod.rs:4074`). `unlink_windows_child_at` opens with delete access (`fs.rs:3528`, called from `remove_entry_at` at `fs.rs:4151`).
